- **principle-anti-bad-excess**: コード生成、リファクタ、ドキュメント作成、回答など、依頼に対して何をどこまでやるか判断するすべての作業で使う。依頼の本質を幹とし、思考のツリーに伸びる追加候補の枝を「過剰」「充実」「必要の更新」に分類して剪定し、「やりすぎ」を防いで出力量を制御する。依頼どおりに進めると明らかな不利益が出る点だけは自律的に補う。 ([seiei-sogen/agent-skills](https://github.com/seiei-sogen/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle/principle-anti-bad-excess))
- **principle-api-and-interface-design**: Designs stable contracts for public interfaces. Use when designing or changing an API — REST or GraphQL endpoints, module or frontend/backend boundaries, component props — or when making a state-changing endpoint safe to retry (idempotency keys). ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-api-and-interface-design) · 2 copies)
- **principle-api-design**: API design principles — contract-first thinking, semantic versioning, idempotency keys, pagination (offset vs cursor), REST vs RPC vs event-driven trade-offs, resource modeling, deprecation strategy. Auto-load when designing an API, adding api versioning, adding pagination, shaping error responses, or evolving a public contract. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-api-design) · ★3)
- **principle-api-design**: Architecture. Apply when designing or reviewing a resource-oriented network API (gRPC/protobuf or Google-style REST): resource naming, standard verbs, pagination, field masks, long-running operations. Not for CLI or internal interface shape. ([Nagato-Yuzuru/eureka-config](https://github.com/Nagato-Yuzuru/eureka-config/tree/HEAD/dot_claude/skills/principle-api-design))
- **principle-architecture**: Guides cross-domain structural decisions before implementation. Use when choosing core data structures, integrating a requirement into an existing design, comparing architectures without precedent, planning a rewrite, retiring internal APIs, or isolating concurrent writers to a file or branch. Service structure and persistence belong to principle-backend, published HTTP contracts to api, and rendered UX to design. The architect workflow owns design sketches and implementation coordination; this skill supplies the structural principles it applies. ([GoBeromsu/craft-skills](https://github.com/GoBeromsu/craft-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-architecture))
- **principle-archival**: Guides treating sources as archival records: finding the true creator behind a republisher (provenance), keeping a source's own order and verbatim text (original order), arranging records from collection to item, and describing a collection as a whole with its coverage and gaps. Use when an article quotes a study, content is reposted or authorship is uncertain, even when another workflow is tracing or recording the source, when tempted to reorder, tidy or replace captured text or to enrich an existing Raw capture, when placing newly captured notes or linking a chapter to its book, or when wri… ([GoBeromsu/secondbrain-skills](https://github.com/GoBeromsu/secondbrain-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-archival) · ★1)
- **principle-arrange-for-the-picture**: 영상의 막과 재질, 서사에 맞춰 palette와 섹션 경계, 강세를 고르는 판단 원칙. 장르 이름보다 화면이 바뀌는 지점과 재질을 먼저 본다. ([ynginkim/op55](https://github.com/ynginkim/op55/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-arrange-for-the-picture))
- **principle-article-over-agent**: Apply before writing any explanation in a byox lesson or concept page. A human-written article, book chapter or video beats an agent's explanation. Link the exact section and write only the gap. ([da5ater/byox](https://github.com/da5ater/byox/tree/HEAD/plugins/byox/skills/principle-article-over-agent))
- **principle-ask-do-not-fill**: Apply when a fact needed for the report or the paper is missing. Write TODO: unknown and ask the user. Never fill it from memory or a guess. ([taixuann/rstack](https://github.com/taixuann/rstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-ask-do-not-fill) · ★1)
- **principle-ask-up-front-then-run**: Apply when tempted to start building before the plan is clear, or tempted to stop mid-build to ask about reversible work. ([Torus403/skills](https://github.com/Torus403/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-ask-up-front-then-run))
- **principle-ask-with-options**: Use before you ask a person a question or for a decision. Give a real example, options with gain, cost and risk, and a recommendation. ([binhle312/cty1ng](https://github.com/binhle312/cty1ng/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-ask-with-options))
- **principle-assemble-the-crew**: Apply when one field is too narrow for the verbs. Pick the fields whose methods the job needs. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-assemble-the-crew))
- **principle-assert-invariants-not-last-bug**: Apply after a bug ships past review or a passing grade/check: assert the general class of failure instead of patching only the specific instance that was caught. Use on /reflect, whenever a fix's own commit message reads like 'fix X for ticker/case Y', or whenever a fix makes a row disappear instead of shipping it with a status. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-assert-invariants-not-last-bug) · ★2)
- **principle-assert-invariants-not-last-bug**: After a holdings/data bug ships past visual QA or a PASS grade: assert the class of failure (no invent, uniqueness, provenance, exits) instead of patching only the named ticker. Use on /reflect, SERV/AUR/DIDIY/BILI-class misses, or when a sheet "looked fine" but a filing says otherwise. ([EdbertChan/hidden_stock](https://github.com/EdbertChan/hidden_stock/tree/HEAD/.cursor/skills/principle-assert-invariants-not-last-bug))
- **principle-assume-the-call-fails**: Apply when code calls anything over a network or another process: an HTTP API, a database, a queue, a cache, a webhook. Give every call a timeout, retry only idempotent work with capped backoff and jitter, and bound every queue and fan-out. ([Torus403/skills](https://github.com/Torus403/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-assume-the-call-fails))
- **principle-async-for-volume**: Apply to anything beyond small data volumes. Move volume out of the synchronous path: queueable for chainable jobs, batch Apex for large data volumes, Bulk API 2.0 for loads, platform events for decoupling. The sync path is for work the user is waiting on. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-async-for-volume) · ★1)
- **principle-atomic-types**: Apply when writing or reviewing TypeScript application functions, module APIs, dependency injection, services, use cases, controllers, or domain logic. Keep application types local and structural: never export type aliases, never declare interfaces, give every function only the smallest invariant-preserving values and capabilities required by its own work and its dependencies, and organize code as a pure functional core with explicit effects at the boundary. ([dakdevs/skills](https://github.com/dakdevs/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-atomic-types))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it. ([elizaOS/eliza](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★20k · 10k installs · 660 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins) · ★17k · 3.2k installs)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Take a census of which actors hold the imbalance before the next fix, then question the premise instead of writing another fix that assumes it. ([nightly-labs/openbot](https://github.com/nightly-labs/openbot/tree/HEAD/marketplace/production-catalog/bundles/pstack/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★9.5k · 224 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 10k installs)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 964 installs)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Use after repeated fixes built on one shared theory of the cause keep failing at the same gate. Stop patching, state that theory in writing, and run a check whose result would change if the theory were wrong. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★1.6k · 5 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when repeated fixes sharing an assumption fail. State the assumption and choose an observation that can challenge it before trying another fix that depends on it. ([chmonitor/chmonitor](https://github.com/chmonitor/chmonitor/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/pstack/upstream/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★305 · 9 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when handed a problem, constraint, or design you are tempted to accept as given. Ask if the premise is false, the constraint is self-imposed, or a simpler framing eliminates the work entirely. ([jtaroreh/agystack](https://github.com/jtaroreh/agystack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★137 · 3 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Inventory who actually owns or exhibits the imbalance, then challenge the shared premise before writing another patch. Use when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★117 · 4 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Take a census of which actors hold the imbalance before the next fix, then question the premise instead of writing another fix that assumes it. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★38)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: WHEN repeated fixes sharing an assumption fail the same check; NOT for a first unexplained failure or a settled mechanical edit; tests the shared premise before another fix. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★37)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when fixes sharing one premise keep failing a gate. ([Cloeille/pstack](https://github.com/Cloeille/pstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★28 · 3 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes sharing one premise have failed the same gate. Write the premise down and test it directly before writing another fix that assumes it. ([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★20 · 7 copies)

Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it. ([patryksztuczka/boberstack](https://github.com/patryksztuczka/boberstack/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★2 · 2 copies)

([pghk/dotfiles](https://github.com/pghk/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/source/private_dot_config/agents/shared/skills/principles/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★2)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: 같은 전제를 공유하는 수정이 둘 이상 같은 게이트에서 실패했을 때 적용한다. 그 전제를 가정하는 수정을 또 쓰기 전에, 전제를 글로 적고 직접 시험한다. ([yjb94/jbstack](https://github.com/yjb94/jbstack/tree/HEAD/core/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · ★2)

- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same check, such as a metric that will not move, a pipeline that keeps failing one assertion, or a model score that stays flat. - **principle-attack-the-premise**: Test the shared assumption before another fix when two attempts fail the same gate. ([alduraimron/agent-first](https://github.com/alduraimron/agent-first/tree/HEAD/plugins/agent-first/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))

- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate, and before a third compensating move against one condition, such as a third raised limit, timeout or retry, or a third redesign after the same outcome. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it. ([MaxFabian25/pstack-harness-adaptations](https://github.com/MaxFabian25/pstack-harness-adaptations/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Question the shared premise before the next fix. For imbalance problems, take a census per actor and test the asymmetry hypothesis. ([MEYD-605/gemini-oracle](https://github.com/MEYD-605/gemini-oracle/tree/HEAD/agy-vibe-kit/skills/principle-attack-the-premise) · 3 copies)
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when multiple fixes have failed the same gate. Suspect the shared premise, not the fixes; test the premise before the next fix, follow the evidence, remove the condition instead of compensating for it. ([PlagueFPS/skills](https://github.com/PlagueFPS/skills/tree/HEAD/pstack/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: 适用于两个或更多共享同一前提的修复在同一个检查关卡上失败时。先把前提写下来，直接检验它，再写下一次默认这个前提的修复。 ([Praying/pstack-zh](https://github.com/Praying/pstack-zh/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: 전제 하나를 공유하는 수정 두 개 이상이 같은 게이트에서 실패했을 때 적용한다. 그 전제를 깔고 가는 수정을 또 쓰기 전에 전제를 적고 직접 검증한다. ([rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko](https://github.com/rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: 1 つの前提を共有する 2 つ以上の修正が同じゲートで失敗したときに適用する。次の修正に進む前に、共有された前提を疑う。不均衡の問題では、アクターごとに実態調査を行い、非対称性の仮説を検証する。 ([seiei-sogen/agent-skills](https://github.com/seiei-sogen/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack-skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it. Explicitly invoke this pstack entry as $principle-attack-the-premise. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when repeated fixes share a premise and fail the same check. State and test that premise before attempting another fix. ([yohannesakd/ticketkit](https://github.com/yohannesakd/ticketkit/tree/HEAD/bundle/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same check. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it. ([YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise**: 两个或更多共用一个前提、又没过同一道闸的修复出现时使用。先把前提写下来并直接检验它，再写下一个假定它成立的修复。 ([zheyi420/Dev-Wiki](https://github.com/zheyi420/Dev-Wiki/tree/HEAD/AI/Agent/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-attack-the-premise))
- **principle-attack-the-premise-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it. ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-attack-the-premise-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-author-is-not-approver**: Apply to every test, test case, plan, or verdict you produced. A draft is done only after a fresh-context reviewer checks it and after you see each new test fail against a broken version. Your own review, passing tests, and CI are not the independent check. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-author-is-not-approver) · ★1)
- **principle-authorize-before-you-look-up**: Apply when adding or reviewing any endpoint, handler, or resolver that takes an identifier from a caller. Decide whether the caller is allowed before you fetch the thing they named, or the difference between your 403 and your 404 tells them what exists. ([lgarceau768/agent-principles](https://github.com/lgarceau768/agent-principles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-authorize-before-you-look-up))
- **principle-automate-checks**: Use when the same correction or review issue repeats, or a rule has several copies. Add a check, or give the rule one home. ([binhle312/cty1ng](https://github.com/binhle312/cty1ng/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-automate-checks))
- **principle-automate-mechanics**: Apply across workflows when choosing between agent judgment, existing tools, and saved scripts. Proactively capture stable, repeatable mechanics as verified automation within the requested scope. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-automate-mechanics))
- **principle-avoid-complexity**: Use when a change would touch many files or places, when code is hard to change safely, when asked how to restructure it, or when names, units, or constants are unclear. Apply even if the user never mentions complexity, coupling, or design, including for conceptual questions. Keeps a system obvious by cutting dependencies and obscurity, with zero tolerance for each small increment of complexity. ([dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness](https://github.com/dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-avoid-complexity))
- **principle-avoid-tautological-tests**: Apply when writing or reviewing automated tests, regression tests, expected values, fixtures, mocks, snapshots, property tests, or coverage-driven test additions. Tautological tests are harmful: require an independent test oracle and a credible way for each test to fail under a plausible defect; reject assertions that merely restate production logic, fixture setup, mock configuration, or the value under test. ([dakdevs/skills](https://github.com/dakdevs/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-avoid-tautological-tests))
- **principle-aws-architecture**: AWS で構成を設計、レビューするときの原則。Well-Architected Framework を土台に、復旧の目標と DR、アカウントの分け方、ネットワーク、ID と権限、データの保護、記録と検知、後から変えられない決定を扱う。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-aws-architecture) · ★10)
- **principle-backend**: Routes backend service architecture and persistence selection, including engine, provider, ORM, production major, roles, destructive-target proof, persistence implementation, schema-ledger retirement, and folder conventions. Use when building a service layer, choosing a persistence stack, adding a repository or use case, setting up production-fidelity local database development, retiring a migration ledger, or reviewing architecture drift (e.g. "백엔드 구조 잡아줘"). After selection, use db for database workload diagnosis, schema/access-path tradeoffs, and operations/configuration. Not for public HTT… ([GoBeromsu/craft-skills](https://github.com/GoBeromsu/craft-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-backend))
- **principle-backend-api-contract**: Apply when adding or changing a backend endpoint, or an asynchronous event/message. Write or update the OpenAPI (sync) or AsyncAPI (async) contract first and generate server types/stubs from it, instead of writing the handler or publisher/consumer freehand. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/backend-mode/skills/principle-backend-api-contract) · ★4)
- **principle-backend-compat**: Apply when changing an existing endpoint's request/response shape, an existing event/message schema, or a database migration. Default to additive changes; version or deprecate anything that would break an existing caller, consumer, or deployed row instead of changing it in place. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/backend-mode/skills/principle-backend-compat) · ★4)
- **principle-backend-layering**: Apply before adding business logic to a handler/route, direct DB/ORM/HTTP access outside a repository or external wrapper, or any code that would duplicate a transport/business-logic/data-access split the project already uses elsewhere. Detect whether that split is already established; if it is, match it. If it isn't, say so and skip rather than scaffolding layers a small project hasn't earned yet. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/backend-mode/skills/principle-backend-layering) · ★4)
- **principle-backend-performance**: Apply when adding a hot request path, a goroutine/worker/background job, or anything that can block a request thread or the event loop. Benchmark before/after on a representative load, bound concurrency instead of spawning it unbounded, and don't block the request path on work that doesn't need to finish before the response. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/backend-mode/skills/principle-backend-performance) · ★4)
- **principle-backend-security**: Apply when adding or touching a request/event boundary, an authn/authz decision, a secret, or a third-party dependency in a backend service. Validate at the edge, enforce the specific least-privilege decision, never hardcode a secret, use parameterized queries, and check new dependencies for known vulnerabilities. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/backend-mode/skills/principle-backend-security) · ★4)
- **principle-backward-compat**: Each commit on main preserves the previous working state. Multi-phase work must revert without taking down the prior phase. Use when stacking commits or changing contracts. ([theb4w/sdd-starter](https://github.com/theb4w/sdd-starter/tree/HEAD/.agent/skills/principle-backward-compat))
- **principle-behavioral-substitutability**: Preserve supported behavior when implementations, inheritance, extension contracts, or mutable domain invariants change. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-behavioral-substitutability) · ★1)
- **principle-behavioral-verification**: Express API and function intent as executable guarantees through the owning boundary; use when writing tests or assessing regression evidence. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-behavioral-verification) · ★1)
- **principle-bind-assigned-ports**: Apply whenever starting a server, choosing a port, or handing back a URL. Ports are assigned, never chosen. Bind 127.0.0.1 explicitly. Anything the operator opens must be forwarded. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-bind-assigned-ports))
- **principle-bind-to-named-inventory**: Apply after an audit, scoring review, or a request for a "real classifier" / "less arbitrary weights" when existing scorers, rankers, or weight formulas were already named. Pick among those named gaps before proposing a new model or library. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-bind-to-named-inventory) · ★2)
- **principle-blackwell**: Blackwell hardware references and rules based on evidence from GPU kernel development. ([para1lel/sglang.cpp](https://github.com/para1lel/sglang.cpp/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-blackwell))
- **principle-blast-radius**: Apply when changing shared code, contracts, permissions, stored data, or asynchronous effects. Identify affected consumers and failure consequences, then scale verification and delivery to that reach. ([alexph/brunel](https://github.com/alexph/brunel/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-blast-radius))
- **principle-block-on-the-human**: Apply when an open question could be answered by the person in front of you. In an interactive planning session the human is the cheapest resolver of ambiguity - ask, and wait. ([mattlindell/photon-plugins](https://github.com/mattlindell/photon-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/ph-plan/skills/principles/principle-block-on-the-human))
- **principle-bound-retries**: Make retries safe, classified, budgeted, and capacity-aware across every execution layer. Mandatory when adding, changing, reviewing, or diagnosing automated retry, redelivery, redrive, or hidden retry layers that affect effects, deadlines, cost, or capacity. ([ElmoJones3/bootes](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/bootes/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-bound-retries) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-bound-telemetry**: Bound collection cost and protect business outcomes when configuring or reviewing telemetry fields, cardinality, queues, exporters, sampling, loss reporting, or shutdown. Does not authorize infrastructure deployment or payload capture. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-bound-telemetry) · ★1)
- **principle-boundaries**: Outside data parsed once, where it enters, strictly into the project's domain types. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-boundaries))
- **principle-boundary-discipline**: Apply when wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Concentrate guards at system boundaries (CLI, config, network, external APIs); trust internal types and keep business logic in pure functions. ([trpc/trpc](https://github.com/trpc/trpc/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-boundary-discipline) · ★41k · 10k installs · 1.1k copies)
- **principle-boundary-discipline**: Apply when wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Concentrate guards at system boundaries (CLI, config, network, external APIs); trust internal types and keep business logic in pure functions. - **principle-boundary-discipline**: Use when wiring input validation, error handling, adapters, CLI or config parsing, or API clients. - **principle-build-the-lever**: 用在任何非平凡的工作上，不只是批量工作：编辑、迁移、分析、检查。造一个做这件事或证明这件事的工具（codemod、脚本、生成器，或一份你的 subagent 会遵守的 skill），不要用手做。工具是评审者能重跑的产物。 ([zheyi420/Dev-Wiki](https://github.com/zheyi420/Dev-Wiki/tree/HEAD/AI/Agent/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-build-the-lever))
- **principle-build-the-lever-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply to any non-trivial work, not just bulk work: edits, migrations, analyses, checks. Build the tool that does it or proves it (codemod, script, generator, or a skill your subagents follow) instead of working by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer can rerun. ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-build-the-lever-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-build-the-shared-model-first**: Use when about to build on someone else's picture (a request, a spec, a resumed plan, another agent's handback), or when agreement on something complex comes too fast. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-build-the-shared-model-first))
- **principle-bulkify-by-default**: Apply to any data operation in Apex or Flow. All operations work on collections; no single-record code paths; trigger and invocable inputs handled as 200-record batches from the first line. Bulkification is necessary but not sufficient for large data volumes. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-bulkify-by-default) · ★1)
- **principle-calibrated-uncertainty**: Apply whenever Jev returns probabilities or confidence, and whenever writing projections, diagnoses, or predictions. Act on high confidence, confirm the middle band, clarify or escalate the low band; never fake certainty. ([frinfo702/cowork](https://github.com/frinfo702/cowork/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-calibrated-uncertainty))
- **principle-caller-obligations**: Simplify API use by moving repeatable mechanics to their owner and comparing language builtins; use for caller setup, ordering, lifecycle, or error duties. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-caller-obligations) · ★1)
- **principle-caption-states-the-conclusion**: Apply when writing a figure caption for a paper or manuscript. State the finding first, then what the elements are, then n, the test and what the error bars mean. ([taixuann/rstack](https://github.com/taixuann/rstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-caption-states-the-conclusion) · ★1)
- **principle-capture-the-baseline**: WHEN about to change something whose result you will verify or compare; NOT for read-only investigation or syncing a git base; records pre-change evidence so the check reads old value versus new value. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-capture-the-baseline) · ★37)
- **principle-central-provider-wiring**: Apply when every feature Provider or call site must pass the same cross-cutting prop (logger, inspector, telemetry, error boundary, auth): wire it once at a root Provider, with per-site overrides. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-central-provider-wiring) · ★1)
- **principle-challenge-from-raw-data**: Apply when the task treats a belief as given. Reproduce that belief from raw material. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-challenge-from-raw-data))
- **principle-challenge-shared-premise**: Question the premise shared by repeatedly failing fixes and choose an observation that can disprove it before another attempt. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-challenge-shared-premise) · ★1)
- **principle-challenge-the-brief**: Apply when a founder states who their customer is, what works, or why deals happen. Check claims against evidence instead of writing them down. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-challenge-the-brief) · ★3)
- **principle-change-conventions-completely**: Decide a local change that proposes a different naming, style, organization, or behavioral convention within an established category. Use when isolated adoption would leave competing conventions active inside the relevant boundary. ([kriscard/Kriscard-Stack](https://github.com/kriscard/Kriscard-Stack/tree/HEAD/skills/dev/principle-change-conventions-completely))
- **principle-change-safety**: Apply before any change to Azure or AWS infrastructure, Terraform, or a GitHub Actions or Azure DevOps pipeline. Read the plan, name the blast radius, make one change at a time and promote it through environments. ([JayCommit/jstack](https://github.com/JayCommit/jstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-change-safety) · ★1)
- **principle-change-sync**: Repeatable workflow for synchronizing APT principle updates across doctrine, checklists, prompts, and references. Use when a principle changes and linked enforcement artifacts must be updated consistently. Triggers: principle update, terminology change, checklist drift, prompt drift, reference contract alignment. ([adthomps/apt-principles-agents](https://github.com/adthomps/apt-principles-agents/tree/HEAD/platforms/github-copilot/source/skills/principle-change-sync))
- **principle-chase-y-not-x**: Apply when a request arrives already shaped as a solution (X): find the real need (Y) behind it before building, and propose a better X when one serves Y. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-chase-y-not-x) · ★1)
- **principle-cheapest-layer-that-catches-it**: Apply when you choose where a test lives. Put each check at the lowest layer where you can observe the failure: static, unit, component or contract, API or integration, end-to-end, then manual or exploratory. Do not repeat one assertion across layers. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cheapest-layer-that-catches-it) · ★1)
- **principle-check**: Grade a proposed feature, scope change, or design decision against felis's four design principles before any code or doc work begins. Returns a per-principle verdict with the exact failing Test clause for any violation. ([felis-terminal/felis](https://github.com/felis-terminal/felis/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-check) · ★9)
- **principle-check-approval**: Use when a fix changes approved behaviour, scope, a contract, or a protected operation such as a migration. Check that an approval covers it. ([binhle312/cty1ng](https://github.com/binhle312/cty1ng/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-check-approval))
- **principle-check-the-facts**: Use before writing assumptions, a spec, a design or a rollout plan: never build on a business fact nobody confirmed. Lists the business facts a decision rests on and sources each from CLAUDE.md, .fbs/context.md or the user; asks the missing or stale ones. ([fabricio21x/fbs](https://github.com/fabricio21x/fbs/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-check-the-facts) · ★1)
- **principle-checklist**: Apply engineering principles as evidence-producing checks at the moment each is cheap — while planning a change (plan), while fixing each review finding (fix), and before commit/PR (finish) — or build a project's own principle list from its PR/MR review history (harvest). Use when starting a non-trivial change, when addressing review feedback, before declaring a change ready, or when a team wants to turn past review comments into checks. ([yuzu-tsuki/claude-skills](https://github.com/yuzu-tsuki/claude-skills/tree/HEAD/principle-checklist))
- **principle-choose-the-numerically-honest-formulation**: Use when choosing formulations, representations, and precision for numerical code. ([xshi19/normix](https://github.com/xshi19/normix/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-choose-the-numerically-honest-formulation))
- **principle-ci-cd-and-automation**: Sets up CI pipelines and their quality gates. Use when creating or modifying a CI pipeline, adding automated checks or test runners to CI, adding preview deploys, or speeding up a slow pipeline. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-ci-cd-and-automation) · 2 copies)
- **principle-cite-or-delete**: Apply when writing any claim in a spec, issue, report, or verdict. Every claim carries its source citation, confidence label, and media where applicable. A claim you cannot cite is deleted, not kept with a hedge. ([jirayusueb/qa-stack](https://github.com/jirayusueb/qa-stack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cite-or-delete))
- **principle-claims-rest-on-records**: Apply before stating any number, status, or citation to the supervisor, when judging a subagent's or tool's report, and when a result looks too good. ([tosunlab/labstack](https://github.com/tosunlab/labstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-claims-rest-on-records) · ★1)
- **principle-classify-before-you-fix**: Apply when a test fails. Name the failure class with evidence before you change anything: product regression, test defect, contract or doc drift, environment, data collision, flaky, or known issue. Do not edit a test or expected value to match the observed behavior unless the class is test defect and the oracle source agrees. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-classify-before-you-fix) · ★1)
- **principle-clean-architecture**: Clean Architecture, hexagonal architecture, ports and adapters, and dependency rule. Auto-load when designing architecture, choosing layers, ports, adapters, or keeping the domain free of framework code. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-clean-architecture) · ★3)
- **principle-clean-architecture-dotnet**: The layered architecture for a .NET system of this class — Domain / Application / Infrastructure with thin composition-root hosts (API, Worker, UI). Defines dependency direction, what each layer may reference, the patterns this standard deliberately omits, and how SOLID is applied. Use when placing a new type, adding a project reference, or deciding where a concern belongs. ([oskarc/AssetHub](https://github.com/oskarc/AssetHub/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-clean-architecture-dotnet) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-clean-code**: Clean code, DRY, KISS, YAGNI, function length, abstraction level, function argument count, Boy Scout Rule, intent-revealing naming. Auto-load when writing functions, reviewing code clarity, or debating whether to abstract. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-clean-code) · ★3)
- **principle-clean-up-what-you-started**: WHEN tearing down after a run, verification or failed attempt; NOT for making reruns converge or reverting unrelated edits; releases only resources this run created and keeps the evidence. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-clean-up-what-you-started) · ★37)
- **principle-clear-naming**: Apply when naming variables, functions, types, files, or endpoints. Enforces the 6-month amnesia test, language conventions, intent-based naming, and cross-boundary consistency. ([Scino/fstack](https://github.com/Scino/fstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-clear-naming) · ★13 · 2 copies)
- **principle-close-or-block-the-dimension**: Apply at the end of any planning session. Every dimension ends either settled or as a named decision ticket - never merely discussed. ([mattlindell/photon-plugins](https://github.com/mattlindell/photon-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/ph-plan/skills/principles/principle-close-or-block-the-dimension))
- **principle-closed-form-over-general-case**: Use when choosing a specialized formula or considering deduplication through a general solver. ([xshi19/normix](https://github.com/xshi19/normix/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-closed-form-over-general-case))
- **principle-code-comment**: コードのコメントに何を書き、何を書かないかを決めるスキル。コメントには原則として Why not（素直な書き方や代案を採らなかった理由）だけを書き、How はコード、What はテスト、Why はコミットメッセージに任せる。 コードを書く・編集するとき、コメントを書く・直す・消すとき、コメントや JSDoc をレビューするとき、「コメント足して」「コメントいる？」「このコメントどう？」と言われたときは必ずこのスキルを使うこと。テストコードのコメントにも使う。 ([kazukit-dev/dotfiles](https://github.com/kazukit-dev/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/skills/principle-code-comment))
- **principle-code-first-documentation**: Treat generated OpenAPI and its documentation UI as part of every HTTP boundary. Mandatory when creating, changing, reviewing, or diagnosing an HTTP server, route, handler, request or response schema, or API error contract. ([ElmoJones3/theseus](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/theseus/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-code-first-documentation) · ★2 · 4 copies)
- **principle-code-mirrors-the-math**: Use when translating a derivation into code or exposing multiple coordinate systems. ([xshi19/normix](https://github.com/xshi19/normix/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-code-mirrors-the-math))
- **principle-code-quality**: Load before writing or changing code in any language, and before reviewing a diff, designing types or interfaces, refactoring, or debugging. Covers cross-language limits, naming, code smells, type and boundary discipline, domain modelling, reader load, deletion-first sequencing, and scope rules, plus on-demand references for Python, TypeScript, C#, GDScript, and Godot. ([Uraxii/dotai](https://github.com/Uraxii/dotai/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-code-quality))
- **principle-code-review**: Code review heuristics — four-axis review lens (correctness, security, design integrity, test coverage); confidence-based filtering (no finding without a concrete failure scenario); review comment tone (observation over accusation); nitpick filtering; what counts as a real finding vs linter noise. Auto-load when writing or framing a review comment, deciding whether a PR finding is worth surfacing, reviewing a diff for correctness, or filtering review nitpicks. ([aibot88/sec_skill_store](https://github.com/aibot88/sec_skill_store/tree/HEAD/skills/claudskills/principle-code-review) · ★6)
- **principle-code-review**: Code review heuristics — five-axis review lens (correctness, security, design integrity, test coverage, comment quality); review comment tone (observation over accusation); nitpick filtering; distinguishing a real finding from linter noise. Auto-load when writing or framing a review comment, reviewing a diff for correctness, or filtering review nitpicks. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-code-review) · ★3)
- **principle-code-simplification**: Simplifies working code without changing behavior. Use when code is harder to read or maintain than it should be, or to deslop a branch of AI-generated code patterns. For prose, use unslop. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-code-simplification) · 2 copies)
- **principle-code-speaks-for-itself**: Apply whenever you write or edit code. Never add a comment that explains the code or your work on it. Say it with names, types, smaller functions, and tests; your work goes in the commit message and the review. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-code-speaks-for-itself))
- **principle-codebase-design**: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-codebase-design) · 2 copies)
- **principle-coding**: 設計・コード・テスト・変更の進め方で判断に迷ったときに参照する、名前で通じる原則の一覧と、原則どうしがぶつかったときの優先順位。コードやテストを書くとき、レビューするときに使う。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-coding) · ★10)
- **principle-color-means-one-thing**: Apply when choosing colours, markers or a colormap for a figure. A condition keeps one colour in every figure, and the figure still reads in greyscale and for colour-blind readers. ([taixuann/rstack](https://github.com/taixuann/rstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-color-means-one-thing) · ★1)
- **principle-column**: Write a short opinion column that names a trend the reader has already noticed, grants it, flips it in one short sentence, defines a principle in two plain sentences, concedes that many reject it, and closes on an assertion plus one hedge. Use this skill for product-management and leadership columns, newsletter opinion pieces, "my take on X" posts, principle explainers, and any 600 to 1,700 word piece for executives and practitioners deciding how to organise; also when the user asks for "a column", "a short opinion piece", "a hot take that still holds up", or "explain the principle behind X".… ([3orionkings/writing-skills](https://github.com/3orionkings/writing-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/writing-skills/skills/principle-column) · ★2)
- **principle-comments**: Keeps a code comment only when another engineer could not understand the code without it. Use when writing, editing, or reviewing a code comment. ([jiyeol-lee/dotfiles](https://github.com/jiyeol-lee/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.opencode/skills/principle-comments))
- **principle-comments-and-docs**: Code & delivery. Apply when writing comments, doc comments, or committed docs. ([Nagato-Yuzuru/eureka-config](https://github.com/Nagato-Yuzuru/eureka-config/tree/HEAD/dot_claude/skills/principle-comments-and-docs))
- **principle-comments-as-design**: Use comments as a design tool to capture abstractions, intent, and decisions code can't express. Use when designing a module, interface, or data structure whose intent won't be obvious from the code alone, and when the user asks to "add comments", "document this", or "explain this code". ([andrezzoid/newsroom](https://github.com/andrezzoid/newsroom/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-comments-as-design))
- **principle-communication**: Caveman output mode — ultra-terse brief replies with fewer tokens. Strips filler, hedging, pleasantries. Three levels — caveman lite (default), caveman full, ultra caveman / max caveman (maximum compression). Persists until normal mode. Auto-load when the user requests terser output, says "be brief", "less tokens", "caveman mode", "use fewer tokens", or invokes `/caveman`. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-communication) · ★3)
- **principle-completion-evidence**: Before claiming completion, directly verify the final artifact or requested behavior and show the evidence supporting that claim. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-completion-evidence) · ★1)
- **principle-compose-over-inherit**: Apply when writing or reviewing a subclass, a base class, or a type hierarchy, or when a new mix of behaviors seems to need a new subclass. Put each varying behavior behind a small interface and combine collaborators at construction. Where inheritance stays, the base class owns the flow and subclasses fill only the hooks it calls. ([thojou/tjstack](https://github.com/thojou/tjstack/tree/HEAD/tjstack/skills/principles/principle-compose-over-inherit))
- **principle-compose-vue-components**: Apply when building or refactoring Vue components. Design accessibility first, organize by responsibility, and express layout variants through composition. ([alexanderop/pstack](https://github.com/alexanderop/pstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-compose-vue-components) · ★1)
- **principle-composition-and-mechanisms**: Keep feature policy at its composition point and extract mechanisms only for independent obligations; use for assembly and interface boundaries. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-composition-and-mechanisms) · ★1)
- **principle-composition-over-flags**: Apply when a component, function or API grows boolean props or mode flags: split it into primitives that compose instead. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-composition-over-flags) · ★1)
- **principle-concept-not-case**: Apply when writing text an agent will read: a skill, an agent, a rule, a CLAUDE.md or a knowledge base file. State the general idea, not the case that taught it. ([geovane-gc/claude-harness](https://github.com/geovane-gc/claude-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principles/principle-concept-not-case))
- **principle-concise**: Apply to any prose you produce: replies, PR descriptions, commit messages, docs, agent-facing instructions. Short declarative sentences, no filler, no bloat; every sentence earns its place by changing a decision. ([RestartDK/dotfiles](https://github.com/RestartDK/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/config/agents/skills/dstack/principle-concise) · ★1)
- **principle-concurrency**: Concurrency principles — race conditions, livelock, backpressure, lock-free primitives, choosing between mutex vs channel vs actor vs semaphore vs CAS. Auto-load when designing concurrent code, fixing a deadlock, choosing a synchronization primitive, or reasoning about goroutine/thread/task lifetimes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-concurrency) · ★3)
- **principle-confidence-from-many-perspectives**: Apply when you are about to call a design, plan, ticket or root-cause claim done, or a change that would be costly if wrong. Ask how confident you are it will work, and get that confidence from independent reviews, not from your own certainty. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-confidence-from-many-perspectives))
- **principle-configuration-values**: Classifies what is safe to commit, what belongs in local tfvars or environment variables, and what deserves a sensible default. Use when authoring or reviewing OpenTofu variables, provider authentication, terraform.tfvars.example, or configuration documentation in this repository. ([lexedwards/homelab](https://github.com/lexedwards/homelab/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-configuration-values) · ★1)
- **principle-conflict**: Run CLAUDE.md's Principle Conflict Protocol when applying an Engineering Principle would conflict with an existing rule, established pattern, or current implementation — including a violation discovered mid-task. Stops work, explains the conflict, and offers three resolution paths for the developer to choose. Use the moment a conflict is noticed, not after picking a side. ([Bluecode77732/Upload-Board-Project](https://github.com/Bluecode77732/Upload-Board-Project/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-conflict) · ★1)
- **principle-constraint-driven-development**: Constraints — a project's quality bar written down in CONSTRAINTS.md with numbers and commands, and guarded against agents lowering it. Use when setting up quality standards or gates (coverage, security, accessibility, performance), when you need a threshold and have no number, or when an agent silences checks, skips tests, or edits thresholds down to get green. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-constraint-driven-development) · 2 copies)
- **principle-constraint-moves**: Apply after relief work ships, a checkpoint fires, or metrics shift. Re-identify the binding constraint. Yesterday's bottleneck is not permanently sacred. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-constraint-moves) · ★6)
- **principle-contain-untrusted-work**: Treat lower-trust data as data and restrict the authority and resources of code, parsers, plugins, tools, and workers that process it. Mandatory when lower-trust input is interpreted or executed, selects a path, URL, query, or command, or receives access to files, networks, secrets, tenants, or sensitive effects. ([ElmoJones3/bootes](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/bootes/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-contain-untrusted-work) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-context-boundaries**: Prefer local authoritative project evidence and retrieve external information only to resolve concrete factual gaps. ([leehosanganson/dotfiles](https://github.com/leehosanganson/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai/.config/ai/skills/principle-context-boundaries))
- **principle-contextualize-and-write-for-the-reader**: Contextualize and write for the reader. Apply when writing anything someone else will read later: a design, a handoff, findings, a report, a verification, a brief, a reply. ([erickb336/sage-stack](https://github.com/erickb336/sage-stack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sage-codex/skills/principle-contextualize-and-write-for-the-reader) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-coordinate-external-effects**: Move durable intent across transaction and ownership boundaries through explicit delivery, reconciliation, and compensation protocols. Mandatory when committed local work must call a provider, publish a message, control a device, send a notification, or otherwise change an external system. ([ElmoJones3/bootes](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/bootes/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-coordinate-external-effects) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-corrections-become-tests**: Apply whenever a human corrects a label, draft, score or claim. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-corrections-become-tests) · ★3)
- **principle-correctness-and-failure-paths**: Trace correctness and failure paths; preserve error context and recovery ownership when logic, guards, errors, or fallbacks change. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-correctness-and-failure-paths) · ★1)
- **principle-correctness-before-speed**: Apply before you time, report, or keep any kernel change. A perf number counts only after an allclose check and a NaN count pass on the same run. ([anhminhnguyenhoang/mstack](https://github.com/anhminhnguyenhoang/mstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-correctness-before-speed) · ★1)
- **principle-correctness-over-pragmatism**: Apply when tempted by a shortcut (a cast, a stub, a guard that masks a crash, a downgraded plan): take the structurally correct fix unless the owner explicitly asked for speed. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-correctness-over-pragmatism) · ★1)
- **principle-cost-aware**: Apply before every tool call and every request to an agent. Everything costs tokens: each turn re-sends the whole context and each request is worked out in full, so do the job in few turns and ask for what the next decision needs. ([geovane-gc/claude-harness](https://github.com/geovane-gc/claude-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principles/principle-cost-aware))
- **principle-cost-aware-delegation**: Apply when delegating to subagents, CI or paid APIs: budget turns and scope before the handoff, front-load context, cap scope, and commit passing work before the budget runs out. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cost-aware-delegation) · ★1)
- **principle-cost-awareness**: Cost awareness principles — FinOps mindset, right-sizing, cost-per-request model, storage tier selection, cardinality explosion, log volume. Auto-load when reasoning about cloud costs, right-sizing instances, scale-to-zero trade-offs, or FinOps budget constraints. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cost-awareness) · ★3)
- **principle-cost-awareness**: Apply before you ship a change that adds or resizes Azure or AWS resources, or that increases log, NAT, public IP or data-transfer use. Price the monthly cost, set a budget alert and drop spend the design does not need. ([JayCommit/jstack](https://github.com/JayCommit/jstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-cost-awareness) · ★1)
- **principle-creation**: Extract reusable decision-making principles from experiences - especially failures - to build a personal or organizational repository of codified learning. ([sethmblack/paks-skills](https://github.com/sethmblack/paks-skills/tree/HEAD/principle-creation) · ★10)
- **principle-customer-data-stays-home**: Apply before any list of people or companies goes to an outside tool, such as an enrichment tool, a scraper or a sender. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-customer-data-stays-home) · ★3)
- **principle-data-modeling**: Data modeling — relational vs document vs KV vs graph selection, normalization depth, indexing strategy, hot-key avoidance, schema evolution via expand–contract, query-first design, retention and archival. Auto-load when designing schemas, schema evolution, indexing strategy, hot-key avoidance, query-first design, or data retention. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-data-modeling) · ★3)
- **principle-data-shape-first**: Use when logic crosses functions or modules ([sansernonline/SQT-Marketplace](https://github.com/sansernonline/SQT-Marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/software-development/.agents/skills/principle-data-shape-first) · 5 copies)
- **principle-ddd**: Domain-Driven Design (DDD) — bounded contexts, entities, domain events, and repository pattern. Auto-load when modeling a complex domain, microservice splits, designing aggregates, or aligning code with business language. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-ddd) · ★3)
- **principle-debugging-and-error-recovery**: Finds and fixes the root cause of a failure. Use when a test fails, a build breaks, a bug is reported, something that used to work regresses, or an unexpected error or wrong behavior appears. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-debugging-and-error-recovery) · 2 copies)
- **principle-decide-then-declare**: Guidance for resolving routine decisions and asking only for consequential missing intent or authority. ([sjdonado/dotfiles](https://github.com/sjdonado/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/modes/sjdonado-mode/skills/principle-decide-then-declare) · ★16)
- **principle-decision-forks**: Apply when about to ask the operator a 'which approach', 'how should I', or 'what should this do' question, or when applying the autonomy grant (autonomy.grant) or an autonomy.always_pause decision. Classify the fork first: observe, investigate, or ask. ([ronicayu/ronica-skills](https://github.com/ronicayu/ronica-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/agentic-team/skills/principle-decision-forks))
- **principle-decision-trail**: Record consequential choices with evidence during long-running, unattended, or multi-session work; preserve pivots without inventing history. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-decision-trail) · ★1)
- **principle-decision-trail**: Apply to long, autonomous, or high-stakes work, and whenever a JEV decision changed the course. Keep a reviewable record of decisions, confidences, and sources. ([frinfo702/cowork](https://github.com/frinfo702/cowork/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-decision-trail))
- **principle-deep-module-design**: Design modules with simple interfaces and rich implementations. Use when creating, extending, or refactoring any module, class, service or API. Trigger on phrases like "simplify the interface", "reduce API surface", "encapsulate", "clean up boundaries", "split this class", "extract a helper", "wrap this", or before writing the first line of a new abstraction. ([andrezzoid/newsroom](https://github.com/andrezzoid/newsroom/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-deep-module-design))
- **principle-deep-modules**: Judge module depth by useful behavior versus caller knowledge and duties; use when evaluating interfaces, hidden mechanics, or seams. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-deep-modules) · ★1)
- **principle-deepdive-html-style**: Generate a single-file deep-dive HTML article in the "principle-deepdive" visual style — dark/light dual-theme, indigo/amber accent palette, inline-SVG hand-drawn diagrams, collapsible cards, citation panels, sticky glass nav, zero external dependencies, offline-readable under 300KB. Use when authoring a long-form analytical HTML article that walks through a topic principle-by-principle with academic citations and visualizations, when the user asks for "principle deep-dive HTML" / "single-file analytical HTML" / "documentation HTML in deepdive style", or when migrating a markdown research not… ([Misakakuroko/principle-deepdive-html-style](https://github.com/Misakakuroko/principle-deepdive-html-style/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md) · ★2)
- **principle-defensive-history**: Recover the incident or contract behind unclear defensive code before removing it; missing rationale is not proof it is unnecessary. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-defensive-history) · ★1)
- **principle-define-done**: WHEN starting a multi-step deliverable, phase or plan, or deciding whether one is finished; NOT for single-step answers or choosing which checks to run; sets a checkable completion criterion first and reports every unmet gate. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-define-done) · ★37)
